# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_SpaceXplore-Core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system provides core algorithms for an interstellar exploration platform. It covers communication reliability, laser and relay transmission, propulsion simulation, solar-sail thrust, autonomous probe navigation, biosignature detection, and sensor data collection. The common theme is lightweight simulation of spacecraft subsystems with deterministic return structures and simple physical models.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Error correction uses a Hamming (7,4) style code. Encoding accepts exactly four binary characters and returns seven bits; invalid input should produce no encoded result. The four data bits occupy positions 1, 2, 4, and 6 of the encoded layout, while parity bits occupy positions 3, 5, and 7. Decoding accepts exactly seven received bits, corrects a single-bit error using the syndrome, and returns the corrected four data bits; invalid received data should produce no decoded result.

Laser communication models signal strength as power scaled by efficiency and linearly reduced by distance, using `power * efficiency / distance`. Transmission succeeds only when the resulting signal strength is above a small threshold of `0.01`.

Ion and nuclear-thermal propulsion share the same simplified operating model. A propulsion unit can activate only when fuel mass is positive. Acceleration is thrust divided by fuel mass while active and zero while inactive. Fuel consumption over time is based on thrust, specific impulse, and standard gravity `9.81`; if fuel would become negative, it should be clamped to zero and the unit should become inactive. Propulsion status maps should use the keys `is_active`, `thrust`, `fuel_mass`, and `acceleration`.

Solar-sail propulsion produces thrust only while deployed. The default solar constant is `1361`, and thrust is proportional to solar constant, sail area, and reflectivity, divided by the speed of light. A non-deployed sail has zero thrust. Solar-sail status maps should use the keys `is_deployed` and `thrust`.

Autonomous navigation works in two-dimensional space. Direction is the normalized vector from current position to destination; if the probe is already at the destination, there is no direction to compute. Movement advances by one speed-scaled step along that direction, and destination checks should use a small numerical tolerance rather than exact floating-point equality.

Biosignature detection is probabilistic. A random detection score in the range `[0.0, 1.0)` is compared against the configured threshold; scores greater than or equal to the threshold count as detected. The higher-level detection action should tolerate arbitrary biosensor payloads without throwing.

Sensor integration should produce realistic bounded readings: temperature in `[-50, 50]`, humidity in `[0, 100]`, pressure in `[900, 1100]`, and a boolean biosensor value. Collected sensor data should use the keys `temperature`, `humidity`, `pressure`, and `biosensor`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate project-specific helper class required outside the UML-defined model.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title spacexplore Model

class AutonomousNavigation {
    TOLERANCE : double
    destination : DoubleArrary
    position : DoubleArrary
    speed : double
    calculateDirection() : DoubleArrary
    hasReachedDestination() : boolean
    move() : void
    navigate() : void
}
class BiosignatureDetection {
    random : Random
    threshold : double
    analyzeData(biosensorData : EJavaObject) : boolean
    detectBiosignature(biosensorData : EJavaObject) : void
}
class ErrorCorrection {
    detectAndCorrect(receivedData : IntArrary) : IntArrary
    hammingCode(data : String) : IntArrary
}
class IonDrive {
    G : double
    fuelMass : double
    isActive : boolean
    specificImpulse : double
    thrust : double
    activate() : void
    calculateAcceleration() : double
    consumeFuel(time : double) : double
    deactivate() : void
    simulate(duration : int) : void
    status() : EMap
}
class LaserCommunication {
    distance : double
    efficiency : double
    power : double
    calculateSignalStrength() : double
    transmitData(data : String) : boolean
}
class NuclearThermal {
    G : double
    fuelMass : double
    isActive : boolean
    specificImpulse : double
    thrust : double
    activate() : void
    calculateAcceleration() : double
    consumeFuel(time : double) : double
    deactivate() : void
    simulate(duration : int) : void
    status() : EMap
}
class RelayNetwork {
    relays : List<String>
    addRelay(relayId : String) : void
    transmitViaRelays(data : String) : boolean
}
class SensorIntegration {
    random : Random
    collectData() : EMap
    readBiosensor() : boolean
    readHumidity() : double
    readPressure() : double
    readTemperature() : double
}
class SolarSail {
    SPEED_OF_LIGHT : double
    area : double
    isDeployed : boolean
    reflectivity : double
    calculateThrust(solarConstant : double) : double
    calculateThrustFixed() : double
    deploy() : void
    retract() : void
    simulate(duration : int) : void
    status() : EMap
}

@enduml
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_SpaceXplore-Core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system provides core algorithms for an interstellar exploration platform. It covers communication reliability, laser and relay transmission, propulsion simulation, solar-sail thrust, autonomous probe navigation, biosignature detection, and sensor data collection. The common theme is lightweight simulation of spacecraft subsystems with deterministic return structures and simple physical models.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Error correction uses a Hamming (7,4) style code. Encoding accepts exactly four binary characters and returns seven bits; invalid input should produce no encoded result. The four data bits occupy positions 1, 2, 4, and 6 of the encoded layout, while parity bits occupy positions 3, 5, and 7. Decoding accepts exactly seven received bits, corrects a single-bit error using the syndrome, and returns the corrected four data bits; invalid received data should produce no decoded result.

Laser communication models signal strength as power scaled by efficiency and linearly reduced by distance, using `power * efficiency / distance`. Transmission succeeds only when the resulting signal strength is above a small threshold of `0.01`.

Ion and nuclear-thermal propulsion share the same simplified operating model. A propulsion unit can activate only when fuel mass is positive. Acceleration is thrust divided by fuel mass while active and zero while inactive. Fuel consumption over time is based on thrust, specific impulse, and standard gravity `9.81`; if fuel would become negative, it should be clamped to zero and the unit should become inactive. Propulsion status maps should use the keys `is_active`, `thrust`, `fuel_mass`, and `acceleration`.

Solar-sail propulsion produces thrust only while deployed. The default solar constant is `1361`, and thrust is proportional to solar constant, sail area, and reflectivity, divided by the speed of light. A non-deployed sail has zero thrust. Solar-sail status maps should use the keys `is_deployed` and `thrust`.

Autonomous navigation works in two-dimensional space. Direction is the normalized vector from current position to destination; if the probe is already at the destination, there is no direction to compute. Movement advances by one speed-scaled step along that direction, and destination checks should use a small numerical tolerance rather than exact floating-point equality.

Biosignature detection is probabilistic. A random detection score in the range `[0.0, 1.0)` is compared against the configured threshold; scores greater than or equal to the threshold count as detected. The higher-level detection action should tolerate arbitrary biosensor payloads without throwing.

Sensor integration should produce realistic bounded readings: temperature in `[-50, 50]`, humidity in `[0, 100]`, pressure in `[900, 1100]`, and a boolean biosensor value. Collected sensor data should use the keys `temperature`, `humidity`, `pressure`, and `biosensor`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate project-specific helper class required outside the UML-defined model.
```

# Operation Specifications
IonDrive::activate
Summary: Activates the ion drive when sufficient fuel is available.
Algorithm:
1. Check fuelMass.
2. If fuelMass > 0, set isActive = true.
3. Otherwise keep or set isActive = false.
Related Features: fuelMass, isActive
Related Classes: None
Input:
- Uses the current fuelMass field.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- fuelMass is initialized to a finite mass value.
Post-condition:
- isActive is true if and only if fuelMass is positive.

IonDrive::calculateAcceleration
Summary: Computes current acceleration from thrust and fuel mass while the drive is active.
Algorithm:
1. If isActive is false or fuelMass <= 0, return 0.
2. Otherwise compute acceleration = thrust / fuelMass.
Related Features: thrust, fuelMass, isActive
Related Classes: None
Input:
- Uses thrust, fuelMass, and isActive of the current object.
Output:
- A double acceleration value.
- Active state acceleration is thrust / fuelMass.
- Inactive state acceleration is 0.
Pre-condition:
- thrust and fuelMass must be finite numeric values.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.

IonDrive::simulate
Summary: Simulates ion drive behavior for the given duration using activation, acceleration, and fuel consumption rules.
Algorithm:
1. If duration <= 0, do nothing.
2. Ensure the drive is activated if fuelMass > 0.
3. For the simulation interval, update fuel using consumeFuel(duration).
4. Recompute or use current acceleration logic as needed.
5. If fuel reaches zero, deactivate.
Related Features: G, fuelMass, isActive, specificImpulse, thrust, activate(), calculateAcceleration(), consumeFuel(), deactivate()
Related Classes: None
Input:
- duration: elapsed simulation time in integer units, expected to be non-negative.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- The propulsion object must have initialized thrust, specificImpulse, fuelMass, and G.
Post-condition:
- Fuel and active state may change according to the simplified propulsion model.

IonDrive::consumeFuel
Summary: Reduces fuel according to thrust, specific impulse, gravity constant, and elapsed time.
Algorithm:
1. If time <= 0 or drive is inactive, return current fuelMass.
2. Compute fuelConsumption = thrust * time / (specificImpulse * G), using G = 9.81.
3. Update fuelMass = max(0, fuelMass - fuelConsumption).
4. If fuelMass becomes 0, set isActive = false.
5. Return updated fuelMass.
Related Features: G, fuelMass, isActive, specificImpulse, thrust
Related Classes: None
Input:
- time: elapsed duration, expected to be non-negative.
Output:
- Updated fuel mass as a double.
- Never negative; clamped to zero.
Pre-condition:
- thrust, specificImpulse, fuelMass, and G must be initialized.
- specificImpulse and G must be positive.
Post-condition:
- fuelMass is decreased according to the simplified model.
- If fuel reaches zero, isActive becomes false.

IonDrive::status
Summary: Returns a status map describing the current ion drive state.
Algorithm:
1. Create a map.
2. Insert key is_active with current isActive.
3. Insert key thrust with current thrust.
4. Insert key fuel_mass with current fuelMass.
5. Insert key acceleration with calculateAcceleration() result.
6. Return the map.
Related Features: isActive, thrust, fuelMass, calculateAcceleration()
Related Classes: EMap
Input:
- None.
Output:
- An EMap containing:
  - is_active: boolean
  - thrust: double
  - fuel_mass: double
  - acceleration: double
Pre-condition:
- The object fields are initialized.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- Returned map uses the required keys.

IonDrive::deactivate
Summary: Deactivates the ion drive.
Algorithm:
1. Set isActive = false.
Related Features: isActive
Related Classes: None
Input:
- None.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- None beyond object initialization.
Post-condition:
- isActive is false.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_SpaceXplore-Core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system provides core algorithms for an interstellar exploration platform. It covers communication reliability, laser and relay transmission, propulsion simulation, solar-sail thrust, autonomous probe navigation, biosignature detection, and sensor data collection. The common theme is lightweight simulation of spacecraft subsystems with deterministic return structures and simple physical models.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Error correction uses a Hamming (7,4) style code. Encoding accepts exactly four binary characters and returns seven bits; invalid input should produce no encoded result. The four data bits occupy positions 1, 2, 4, and 6 of the encoded layout, while parity bits occupy positions 3, 5, and 7. Decoding accepts exactly seven received bits, corrects a single-bit error using the syndrome, and returns the corrected four data bits; invalid received data should produce no decoded result.

Laser communication models signal strength as power scaled by efficiency and linearly reduced by distance, using `power * efficiency / distance`. Transmission succeeds only when the resulting signal strength is above a small threshold of `0.01`.

Ion and nuclear-thermal propulsion share the same simplified operating model. A propulsion unit can activate only when fuel mass is positive. Acceleration is thrust divided by fuel mass while active and zero while inactive. Fuel consumption over time is based on thrust, specific impulse, and standard gravity `9.81`; if fuel would become negative, it should be clamped to zero and the unit should become inactive. Propulsion status maps should use the keys `is_active`, `thrust`, `fuel_mass`, and `acceleration`.

Solar-sail propulsion produces thrust only while deployed. The default solar constant is `1361`, and thrust is proportional to solar constant, sail area, and reflectivity, divided by the speed of light. A non-deployed sail has zero thrust. Solar-sail status maps should use the keys `is_deployed` and `thrust`.

Autonomous navigation works in two-dimensional space. Direction is the normalized vector from current position to destination; if the probe is already at the destination, there is no direction to compute. Movement advances by one speed-scaled step along that direction, and destination checks should use a small numerical tolerance rather than exact floating-point equality.

Biosignature detection is probabilistic. A random detection score in the range `[0.0, 1.0)` is compared against the configured threshold; scores greater than or equal to the threshold count as detected. The higher-level detection action should tolerate arbitrary biosensor payloads without throwing.

Sensor integration should produce realistic bounded readings: temperature in `[-50, 50]`, humidity in `[0, 100]`, pressure in `[900, 1100]`, and a boolean biosensor value. Collected sensor data should use the keys `temperature`, `humidity`, `pressure`, and `biosensor`.
```

# Operation Specifications
IonDrive::activate
Summary: Activates the ion drive when sufficient fuel is available.
Algorithm:
1. Check fuelMass.
2. If fuelMass > 0, set isActive = true.
3. Otherwise keep or set isActive = false.
Related Features: fuelMass, isActive
Related Classes: None
Input:
- Uses the current fuelMass field.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- fuelMass is initialized to a finite mass value.
Post-condition:
- isActive is true if and only if fuelMass is positive.

IonDrive::calculateAcceleration
Summary: Computes current acceleration from thrust and fuel mass while the drive is active.
Algorithm:
1. If isActive is false or fuelMass <= 0, return 0.
2. Otherwise compute acceleration = thrust / fuelMass.
Related Features: thrust, fuelMass, isActive
Related Classes: None
Input:
- Uses thrust, fuelMass, and isActive of the current object.
Output:
- A double acceleration value.
- Active state acceleration is thrust / fuelMass.
- Inactive state acceleration is 0.
Pre-condition:
- thrust and fuelMass must be finite numeric values.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.

IonDrive::simulate
Summary: Simulates ion drive behavior for the given duration using activation, acceleration, and fuel consumption rules.
Algorithm:
1. If duration <= 0, do nothing.
2. If fuelMass > 0, set isActive = true; otherwise set isActive = false.
3. If isActive is true, update fuelMass by calling consumeFuel(duration).
4. If fuelMass becomes 0 after consumption, deactivate the drive.
5. Acceleration is determined by calculateAcceleration() when needed by the simulation logic.
Related Features: G, fuelMass, isActive, specificImpulse, thrust, activate(), calculateAcceleration(), consumeFuel(), deactivate()
Related Classes: None
Input:
- duration: elapsed simulation time in integer units, expected to be non-negative.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- The propulsion object must have initialized thrust, specificImpulse, fuelMass, and G.
Post-condition:
- Fuel and active state may change according to the simplified propulsion model.

IonDrive::consumeFuel
Summary: Reduces fuel according to thrust, specific impulse, gravity constant, and elapsed time.
Algorithm:
1. If time <= 0 or drive is inactive, return current fuelMass.
2. Compute fuelConsumption = thrust * time / (specificImpulse * G), using G = 9.81.
3. Update fuelMass = max(0, fuelMass - fuelConsumption).
4. If fuelMass becomes 0, set isActive = false.
5. Return updated fuelMass.
Related Features: G, fuelMass, isActive, specificImpulse, thrust
Related Classes: None
Input:
- time: elapsed duration, expected to be non-negative.
Output:
- Updated fuel mass as a double.
- Never negative; clamped to zero.
Pre-condition:
- thrust, specificImpulse, fuelMass, and G must be initialized.
- specificImpulse and G must be positive.
Post-condition:
- fuelMass is decreased according to the simplified model.
- If fuel reaches zero, isActive becomes false.

IonDrive::status
Summary: Returns a status map describing the current ion drive state.
Algorithm:
1. Create a map.
2. Insert key is_active with current isActive.
3. Insert key thrust with current thrust.
4. Insert key fuel_mass with current fuelMass.
5. Insert key acceleration with calculateAcceleration() result.
6. Return the map.
Related Features: isActive, thrust, fuelMass, calculateAcceleration()
Related Classes: EMap
Input:
- None.
Output:
- An EMap containing:
  - is_active: boolean
  - thrust: double
  - fuel_mass: double
  - acceleration: double
Pre-condition:
- The object fields are initialized.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- Returned map uses the required keys.

IonDrive::deactivate
Summary: Deactivates the ion drive.
Algorithm:
1. Set isActive = false.
Related Features: isActive
Related Classes: None
Input:
- None.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- None beyond object initialization.
Post-condition:
- isActive is false.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_SpaceXplore-Core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system provides core algorithms for an interstellar exploration platform. It covers communication reliability, laser and relay transmission, propulsion simulation, solar-sail thrust, autonomous probe navigation, biosignature detection, and sensor data collection. The common theme is lightweight simulation of spacecraft subsystems with deterministic return structures and simple physical models.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Error correction uses a Hamming (7,4) style code. Encoding accepts exactly four binary characters and returns seven bits; invalid input should produce no encoded result. The four data bits occupy positions 1, 2, 4, and 6 of the encoded layout, while parity bits occupy positions 3, 5, and 7. Decoding accepts exactly seven received bits, corrects a single-bit error using the syndrome, and returns the corrected four data bits; invalid received data should produce no decoded result.

Laser communication models signal strength as power scaled by efficiency and linearly reduced by distance, using `power * efficiency / distance`. Transmission succeeds only when the resulting signal strength is above a small threshold of `0.01`.

Ion and nuclear-thermal propulsion share the same simplified operating model. A propulsion unit can activate only when fuel mass is positive. Acceleration is thrust divided by fuel mass while active and zero while inactive. Fuel consumption over time is based on thrust, specific impulse, and standard gravity `9.81`; if fuel would become negative, it should be clamped to zero and the unit should become inactive. Propulsion status maps should use the keys `is_active`, `thrust`, `fuel_mass`, and `acceleration`.

Solar-sail propulsion produces thrust only while deployed. The default solar constant is `1361`, and thrust is proportional to solar constant, sail area, and reflectivity, divided by the speed of light. A non-deployed sail has zero thrust. Solar-sail status maps should use the keys `is_deployed` and `thrust`.

Autonomous navigation works in two-dimensional space. Direction is the normalized vector from current position to destination; if the probe is already at the destination, there is no direction to compute. Movement advances by one speed-scaled step along that direction, and destination checks should use a small numerical tolerance rather than exact floating-point equality.

Biosignature detection is probabilistic. A random detection score in the range `[0.0, 1.0)` is compared against the configured threshold; scores greater than or equal to the threshold count as detected. The higher-level detection action should tolerate arbitrary biosensor payloads without throwing.

Sensor integration should produce realistic bounded readings: temperature in `[-50, 50]`, humidity in `[0, 100]`, pressure in `[900, 1100]`, and a boolean biosensor value. Collected sensor data should use the keys `temperature`, `humidity`, `pressure`, and `biosensor`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate project-specific helper class required outside the UML-defined model.
```

# Operation Specifications
LaserCommunication::calculateSignalStrength
Summary: Computes received laser signal strength using power, efficiency, and distance.
Algorithm:
1. If distance <= 0, return 0 or an implementation-defined safe non-positive-distance handling result.
2. Compute signalStrength = power * efficiency / distance.
Related Features: distance, efficiency, power
Related Classes: None
Input:
- Uses current power, efficiency, and distance fields.
Output:
- A double signal strength value.
Pre-condition:
- power and efficiency are finite numeric values.
- distance should be positive to avoid division by zero.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.

LaserCommunication::transmitData
Summary: Determines whether laser transmission succeeds based on signal strength threshold.
Algorithm:
1. Compute signal strength using calculateSignalStrength().
2. Return true if signal strength > 0.01.
3. Otherwise return false.
Related Features: distance, efficiency, power, calculateSignalStrength()
Related Classes: None
Input:
- data: payload string to transmit.
- The payload content does not affect the success rule in the simplified model.
Output:
- boolean indicating transmission success.
Pre-condition:
- power, efficiency, and distance must be initialized.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- Success depends only on calculated signal strength exceeding 0.01.

```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_SpaceXplore-Core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system provides core algorithms for an interstellar exploration platform. It covers communication reliability, laser and relay transmission, propulsion simulation, solar-sail thrust, autonomous probe navigation, biosignature detection, and sensor data collection. The common theme is lightweight simulation of spacecraft subsystems with deterministic return structures and simple physical models.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Error correction uses a Hamming (7,4) style code. Encoding accepts exactly four binary characters and returns seven bits; invalid input should produce no encoded result. The four data bits occupy positions 1, 2, 4, and 6 of the encoded layout, while parity bits occupy positions 3, 5, and 7. Decoding accepts exactly seven received bits, corrects a single-bit error using the syndrome, and returns the corrected four data bits; invalid received data should produce no decoded result.

Laser communication models signal strength as power scaled by efficiency and linearly reduced by distance, using `power * efficiency / distance`. Transmission succeeds only when the resulting signal strength is above a small threshold of `0.01`.

Ion and nuclear-thermal propulsion share the same simplified operating model. A propulsion unit can activate only when fuel mass is positive. Acceleration is thrust divided by fuel mass while active and zero while inactive. Fuel consumption over time is based on thrust, specific impulse, and standard gravity `9.81`; if fuel would become negative, it should be clamped to zero and the unit should become inactive. Propulsion status maps should use the keys `is_active`, `thrust`, `fuel_mass`, and `acceleration`.

Solar-sail propulsion produces thrust only while deployed. The default solar constant is `1361`, and thrust is proportional to solar constant, sail area, and reflectivity, divided by the speed of light. A non-deployed sail has zero thrust. Solar-sail status maps should use the keys `is_deployed` and `thrust`.

Autonomous navigation works in two-dimensional space. Direction is the normalized vector from current position to destination; if the probe is already at the destination, there is no direction to compute. Movement advances by one speed-scaled step along that direction, and destination checks should use a small numerical tolerance rather than exact floating-point equality.

Biosignature detection is probabilistic. A random detection score in the range `[0.0, 1.0)` is compared against the configured threshold; scores greater than or equal to the threshold count as detected. The higher-level detection action should tolerate arbitrary biosensor payloads without throwing.

Sensor integration should produce realistic bounded readings: temperature in `[-50, 50]`, humidity in `[0, 100]`, pressure in `[900, 1100]`, and a boolean biosensor value. Collected sensor data should use the keys `temperature`, `humidity`, `pressure`, and `biosensor`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate project-specific helper class required outside the UML-defined model.
```

```plantuml
@startuml

title spacexplore Model

class AutonomousNavigation {
    TOLERANCE : double
    destination : DoubleArrary
    position : DoubleArrary
    speed : double

    navigate() : void
}

    random : Random
    threshold : double
    analyzeData(biosensorData : EJavaObject) : boolean
    detectBiosignature(biosensorData : EJavaObject) : void
}

    detectAndCorrect(receivedData : IntArrary) : IntArrary
    hammingCode(data : String) : IntArrary
}
class IonDrive {
    G : double
    fuelMass : double
    isActive : boolean
    specificImpulse : double
    thrust : double
    activate() : void
    calculateAcceleration() : double
    consumeFuel(time : double) : double
    deactivate() : void
    simulate(duration : int) : void
    status() : EMap
}

    distance : double
    efficiency : double
    power : double
    calculateSignalStrength() : double
    transmitData(data : String) : boolean
}

    G : double
    fuelMass : double
    isActive : boolean
    specificImpulse : double
    thrust : double
    activate() : void

    relays : List<String>

    random : Random
    collectData() : EMap

    readHumidity() : double
    readPressure() : double
    readTemperature() : double
}
class SolarSail {
    SPEED_OF_LIGHT : double
    area : double
    isDeployed : boolean
    reflectivity : double
    calculateThrust(solarConstant : double) : double
    calculateThrustFixed() : double
    deploy() : void
    retract() : void
    simulate(duration : int) : void
    status() : EMap
}

@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
LaserCommunication::calculateSignalStrength
Summary: Computes received laser signal strength using power, efficiency, and distance.
Algorithm:
1. If distance <= 0, return 0 or an implementation-defined safe non-positive-distance handling result.
2. Compute signalStrength = power * efficiency / distance.
Related Features: distance, efficiency, power
Related Classes: None
Input:
- Uses current power, efficiency, and distance fields.
Output:
- A double signal strength value.
Pre-condition:
- power and efficiency are finite numeric values.
- distance should be positive to avoid division by zero.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.

LaserCommunication::transmitData
Summary: Determines whether laser transmission succeeds based on signal strength threshold.
Algorithm:
1. Compute signal strength using calculateSignalStrength().
2. Return true if signal strength > 0.01.
3. Otherwise return false.
Related Features: distance, efficiency, power, calculateSignalStrength()
Related Classes: None
Input:
- data: payload string to transmit.
- The payload content does not affect the success rule in the simplified model.
Output:
- boolean indicating transmission success.
Pre-condition:
- power, efficiency, and distance must be initialized.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- Success depends only on calculated signal strength exceeding 0.01.

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Operation Specifications
AutonomousNavigation::calculateDirection
Summary: Computes the normalized 2D direction vector from the current position to the destination.
Algorithm:
1. Read position and destination as 2D numeric arrays.
2. Compute delta = destination - position.
3. Compute magnitude = sqrt(delta.x^2 + delta.y^2).
4. If magnitude is zero or below tolerance, return no direction result in the implementation-defined empty/null form.
5. Otherwise return normalized direction = delta / magnitude.
Related Features: destination, position, TOLERANCE
Related Classes: None
Input:
- Uses the current object's position and destination fields.
- position and destination are expected to be 2-element arrays of doubles.
Output:
- A 2-element normalized direction vector as DoubleArrary.
- If current position equals destination within tolerance, no meaningful direction is produced.
Pre-condition:
- position and destination must both be initialized 2D coordinates.
- Their lengths must match and contain finite numeric values.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- Returned vector, if present, has unit length within numerical tolerance.

AutonomousNavigation::move
Summary: Advances the probe by one step along the current direction toward the destination.
Algorithm:
1. If hasReachedDestination() is true, do nothing.
2. Otherwise compute direction using calculateDirection().
3. Update position = position + direction * speed.
4. If the updated position overshoots the destination due to step size, the implementation may leave the value as computed or rely on tolerance-based reached checks.
Related Features: destination, position, speed, TOLERANCE, calculateDirection(), hasReachedDestination()
Related Classes: None
Input:
- Uses the current object's speed, position, and destination fields.
- speed is a non-negative scalar step multiplier.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- position, destination, and speed must be initialized.
- speed must be finite and not negative.
Post-condition:
- If not already at destination, position is advanced in the direction of destination.
- If already at destination, position remains unchanged.

AutonomousNavigation::hasReachedDestination
Summary: Checks whether the current position is close enough to the destination using the configured tolerance.
Algorithm:
1. Compute the absolute difference between position and destination on each coordinate.
2. Compute distance = sqrt(dx^2 + dy^2).
3. Return true if distance <= TOLERANCE, otherwise false.
Related Features: destination, position, TOLERANCE
Related Classes: None
Input:
- Uses the current object's position and destination fields.
Output:
- boolean indicating whether the destination has been reached within tolerance.
Pre-condition:
- position and destination must be initialized 2D coordinates of equal length.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.

AutonomousNavigation::navigate
Summary: Performs a single navigation cycle by checking destination status and moving if needed.
Algorithm:
1. Call hasReachedDestination().
2. If true, stop.
3. Otherwise call move().
Related Features: destination, position, speed, TOLERANCE, calculateDirection(), hasReachedDestination(), move()
Related Classes: None
Input:
- Uses the current object's navigation state.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- position and destination must be initialized 2D coordinates.
Post-condition:
- The object either remains at destination or is moved one step closer to it.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_SpaceXplore-Core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system provides core algorithms for an interstellar exploration platform. It covers communication reliability, laser and relay transmission, propulsion simulation, solar-sail thrust, autonomous probe navigation, biosignature detection, and sensor data collection. The common theme is lightweight simulation of spacecraft subsystems with deterministic return structures and simple physical models.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Error correction uses a Hamming (7,4) style code. Encoding accepts exactly four binary characters and returns seven bits; invalid input should produce no encoded result. The four data bits occupy positions 1, 2, 4, and 6 of the encoded layout, while parity bits occupy positions 3, 5, and 7. Decoding accepts exactly seven received bits, corrects a single-bit error using the syndrome, and returns the corrected four data bits; invalid received data should produce no decoded result.

Laser communication models signal strength as power scaled by efficiency and linearly reduced by distance, using `power * efficiency / distance`. Transmission succeeds only when the resulting signal strength is above a small threshold of `0.01`.

Ion and nuclear-thermal propulsion share the same simplified operating model. A propulsion unit can activate only when fuel mass is positive. Acceleration is thrust divided by fuel mass while active and zero while inactive. Fuel consumption over time is based on thrust, specific impulse, and standard gravity `9.81`; if fuel would become negative, it should be clamped to zero and the unit should become inactive. Propulsion status maps should use the keys `is_active`, `thrust`, `fuel_mass`, and `acceleration`.

Solar-sail propulsion produces thrust only while deployed. The default solar constant is `1361`, and thrust is proportional to solar constant, sail area, and reflectivity, divided by the speed of light. A non-deployed sail has zero thrust. Solar-sail status maps should use the keys `is_deployed` and `thrust`.

Autonomous navigation works in two-dimensional space. Direction is the normalized vector from current position to destination; if the probe is already at the destination, there is no direction to compute. Movement advances by one speed-scaled step along that direction, and destination checks should use a small numerical tolerance rather than exact floating-point equality.

Biosignature detection is probabilistic. A random detection score in the range `[0.0, 1.0)` is compared against the configured threshold; scores greater than or equal to the threshold count as detected. The higher-level detection action should tolerate arbitrary biosensor payloads without throwing.

Sensor integration should produce realistic bounded readings: temperature in `[-50, 50]`, humidity in `[0, 100]`, pressure in `[900, 1100]`, and a boolean biosensor value. Collected sensor data should use the keys `temperature`, `humidity`, `pressure`, and `biosensor`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate project-specific helper class required outside the UML-defined model.
```

# Operation Specifications
BiosignatureDetection::analyzeData
Summary: Evaluates biosensor payload probabilistically and decides whether a biosignature is detected.
Algorithm:
1. Generate a random score r in [0.0, 1.0).
2. Compare r with threshold.
3. Return true if r >= threshold; otherwise return false.
4. If biosensorData is malformed or arbitrary, ignore its contents and still perform the comparison without throwing.
Related Features: random, threshold
Related Classes: None
Input:
- biosensorData: arbitrary payload object; may be null or any type.
- threshold: detection threshold, expected in [0.0, 1.0].
Output:
- boolean detection result.
Pre-condition:
- threshold should be a finite probability threshold in [0.0, 1.0].
Post-condition:
- No exception is thrown for arbitrary biosensorData.
- Internal random generator state may advance.

BiosignatureDetection::detectBiosignature
Summary: High-level biosignature detection action that delegates to probabilistic analysis.
Algorithm:
1. Call analyzeData(biosensorData).
2. Interpret the returned boolean as the detection outcome.
3. Perform any implementation-defined side effect such as reporting/logging, if present, without altering the required detection rule.
Related Features: random, threshold, analyzeData()
Related Classes: None
Input:
- biosensorData: arbitrary payload object; may be null.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- The detection instance must be initialized with a valid threshold and random source.
Post-condition:
- The detection result is produced by analyzeData and tolerated for arbitrary payloads.

# Operation Specifications
SolarSail::calculateThrust
Summary: Computes solar-sail thrust from solar constant, sail area, and reflectivity.
Algorithm:
1. If the sail is not deployed, return 0.
2. Otherwise compute thrust = solarConstant * area * reflectivity / SPEED_OF_LIGHT.
3. Return the computed thrust.
Related Features: SPEED_OF_LIGHT, area, isDeployed, reflectivity
Related Classes: None
Input:
- solarConstant: incident solar energy constant, expected positive; default operational value is 1361 when used by the fixed variant.
Output:
- A double thrust value.
- Zero when not deployed.
Pre-condition:
- area, reflectivity, and SPEED_OF_LIGHT are initialized.
- SPEED_OF_LIGHT must be positive.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.

SolarSail::deploy
Summary: Deploys the solar sail.
Algorithm:
1. Set isDeployed = true.
Related Features: isDeployed
Related Classes: None
Input:
- None.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- None beyond object initialization.
Post-condition:
- isDeployed is true.

SolarSail::retract
Summary: Retracts the solar sail.
Algorithm:
1. Set isDeployed = false.
Related Features: isDeployed
Related Classes: None
Input:
- None.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- None beyond object initialization.
Post-condition:
- isDeployed is false.

SolarSail::simulate
Summary: Simulates the solar-sail state over a duration, preserving the deployment rule for thrust generation.
Algorithm:
1. If duration <= 0, do nothing.
2. If deployed, compute thrust using the solar-sail model and the default solar constant 1361.
3. If not deployed, thrust remains zero.
4. The simulation may refresh derived thrust-related outputs without changing deployment rules.
Related Features: SPEED_OF_LIGHT, area, isDeployed, reflectivity, calculateThrust(), calculateThrustFixed(), deploy(), retract()
Related Classes: None
Input:
- duration: simulation duration in integer units, non-negative.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- area, reflectivity, and SPEED_OF_LIGHT are initialized.
Post-condition:
- Deployment state may be preserved; derived thrust behavior follows deployed/non-deployed rule.

SolarSail::status
Summary: Returns a status map describing the current solar-sail state.
Algorithm:
1. Create a map.
2. Insert key is_deployed with current isDeployed.
3. Insert key thrust with the current thrust value, computed using the model if necessary.
4. Return the map.
Related Features: isDeployed, calculateThrust(), calculateThrustFixed(), SPEED_OF_LIGHT, area, reflectivity
Related Classes: EMap
Input:
- None.
Output:
- An EMap containing:
  - is_deployed: boolean
  - thrust: double
Pre-condition:
- The sail state and physical parameters are initialized.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- Returned map uses the required keys.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_SpaceXplore-Core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system provides core algorithms for an interstellar exploration platform. It covers communication reliability, laser and relay transmission, propulsion simulation, solar-sail thrust, autonomous probe navigation, biosignature detection, and sensor data collection. The common theme is lightweight simulation of spacecraft subsystems with deterministic return structures and simple physical models.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Error correction uses a Hamming (7,4) style code. Encoding accepts exactly four binary characters and returns seven bits; invalid input should produce no encoded result. The four data bits occupy positions 1, 2, 4, and 6 of the encoded layout, while parity bits occupy positions 3, 5, and 7. Decoding accepts exactly seven received bits, corrects a single-bit error using the syndrome, and returns the corrected four data bits; invalid received data should produce no decoded result.

Laser communication models signal strength as power scaled by efficiency and linearly reduced by distance, using `power * efficiency / distance`. Transmission succeeds only when the resulting signal strength is above a small threshold of `0.01`.

Ion and nuclear-thermal propulsion share the same simplified operating model. A propulsion unit can activate only when fuel mass is positive. Acceleration is thrust divided by fuel mass while active and zero while inactive. Fuel consumption over time is based on thrust, specific impulse, and standard gravity `9.81`; if fuel would become negative, it should be clamped to zero and the unit should become inactive. Propulsion status maps should use the keys `is_active`, `thrust`, `fuel_mass`, and `acceleration`.

Solar-sail propulsion produces thrust only while deployed. The default solar constant is `1361`, and thrust is proportional to solar constant, sail area, and reflectivity, divided by the speed of light. A non-deployed sail has zero thrust. Solar-sail status maps should use the keys `is_deployed` and `thrust`.

Autonomous navigation works in two-dimensional space. Direction is the normalized vector from current position to destination; if the probe is already at the destination, there is no direction to compute. Movement advances by one speed-scaled step along that direction, and destination checks should use a small numerical tolerance rather than exact floating-point equality.

Biosignature detection is probabilistic. A random detection score in the range `[0.0, 1.0)` is compared against the configured threshold; scores greater than or equal to the threshold count as detected. The higher-level detection action should tolerate arbitrary biosensor payloads without throwing.

Sensor integration should produce realistic bounded readings: temperature in `[-50, 50]`, humidity in `[0, 100]`, pressure in `[900, 1100]`, and a boolean biosensor value. Collected sensor data should use the keys `temperature`, `humidity`, `pressure`, and `biosensor`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate project-specific helper class required outside the UML-defined model.
```

# Operation Specifications
SolarSail::calculateThrust
Summary: Computes solar-sail thrust from solar constant, sail area, and reflectivity.
Algorithm:
1. If the sail is not deployed, return 0.
2. Otherwise compute thrust = solarConstant * area * reflectivity / SPEED_OF_LIGHT.
3. Return the computed thrust.
Related Features: SPEED_OF_LIGHT, area, isDeployed, reflectivity
Related Classes: None
Input:
- solarConstant: incident solar energy constant, expected positive; default operational value is 1361 when used by the fixed variant.
Output:
- A double thrust value.
- Zero when not deployed.
Pre-condition:
- area, reflectivity, and SPEED_OF_LIGHT are initialized.
- SPEED_OF_LIGHT must be positive.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.

SolarSail::deploy
Summary: Deploys the solar sail.
Algorithm:
1. Set isDeployed = true.
Related Features: isDeployed
Related Classes: None
Input:
- None.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- None beyond object initialization.
Post-condition:
- isDeployed is true.

SolarSail::retract
Summary: Retracts the solar sail.
Algorithm:
1. Set isDeployed = false.
Related Features: isDeployed
Related Classes: None
Input:
- None.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- None beyond object initialization.
Post-condition:
- isDeployed is false.

SolarSail::simulate
Summary: Simulates the solar-sail state over a duration, preserving the deployment rule for thrust generation.
Algorithm:
1. If duration <= 0, do nothing.
2. If deployed, compute thrust using the solar-sail model and the default solar constant 1361.
3. If not deployed, thrust remains zero.
4. The simulation may refresh derived thrust-related outputs without changing deployment rules.
Related Features: SPEED_OF_LIGHT, area, isDeployed, reflectivity, calculateThrust(), calculateThrustFixed(), deploy(), retract()
Related Classes: None
Input:
- duration: simulation duration in integer units, non-negative.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- area, reflectivity, and SPEED_OF_LIGHT are initialized.
Post-condition:
- Deployment state may be preserved; derived thrust behavior follows deployed/non-deployed rule.

SolarSail::status
Summary: Returns a status map describing the current solar-sail state.
Algorithm:
1. Create a map.
2. Insert key is_deployed with current isDeployed.
3. Insert key thrust with the current thrust value, computed using the model if necessary.
4. Return the map.
Related Features: isDeployed, calculateThrust(), calculateThrustFixed(), SPEED_OF_LIGHT, area, reflectivity
Related Classes: EMap
Input:
- None.
Output:
- An EMap containing:
  - is_deployed: boolean
  - thrust: double
Pre-condition:
- The sail state and physical parameters are initialized.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- Returned map uses the required keys.

# Operation Specifications
RelayNetwork::addRelay
Summary: Adds a relay identifier to the relay list.
Algorithm:
1. Validate relayId as a string identifier.
2. Append relayId to relays.
3. If duplicates are allowed by implementation, append as-is; otherwise ignore duplicates.
Related Features: relays
Related Classes: None
Input:
- relayId: relay identifier string.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- relays list is initialized.
- relayId is not null or empty for meaningful use.
Post-condition:
- relays contains the added relay identifier.

RelayNetwork::transmitViaRelays
Summary: Attempts to transmit data through the configured relay chain.
Algorithm:
1. If relays is empty, return false.
2. Iterate through relay identifiers in order.
3. Apply a simplified pass-through rule per relay.
4. If all relay steps succeed, return true; otherwise return false.
5. The data payload is carried unchanged in this simplified model.
Related Features: relays, addRelay()
Related Classes: None
Input:
- data: payload string to transmit.
Output:
- boolean indicating whether transmission through relays succeeds.
Pre-condition:
- relays is initialized.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.
- Success depends on the relay list configuration.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_SpaceXplore-Core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system provides core algorithms for an interstellar exploration platform. It covers communication reliability, laser and relay transmission, propulsion simulation, solar-sail thrust, autonomous probe navigation, biosignature detection, and sensor data collection. The common theme is lightweight simulation of spacecraft subsystems with deterministic return structures and simple physical models.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Error correction uses a Hamming (7,4) style code. Encoding accepts exactly four binary characters and returns seven bits; invalid input should produce no encoded result. The four data bits occupy positions 1, 2, 4, and 6 of the encoded layout, while parity bits occupy positions 3, 5, and 7. Decoding accepts exactly seven received bits, corrects a single-bit error using the syndrome, and returns the corrected four data bits; invalid received data should produce no decoded result.

Laser communication models signal strength as power scaled by efficiency and linearly reduced by distance, using `power * efficiency / distance`. Transmission succeeds only when the resulting signal strength is above a small threshold of `0.01`.

Ion and nuclear-thermal propulsion share the same simplified operating model. A propulsion unit can activate only when fuel mass is positive. Acceleration is thrust divided by fuel mass while active and zero while inactive. Fuel consumption over time is based on thrust, specific impulse, and standard gravity `9.81`; if fuel would become negative, it should be clamped to zero and the unit should become inactive. Propulsion status maps should use the keys `is_active`, `thrust`, `fuel_mass`, and `acceleration`.

Solar-sail propulsion produces thrust only while deployed. The default solar constant is `1361`, and thrust is proportional to solar constant, sail area, and reflectivity, divided by the speed of light. A non-deployed sail has zero thrust. Solar-sail status maps should use the keys `is_deployed` and `thrust`.

Autonomous navigation works in two-dimensional space. Direction is the normalized vector from current position to destination; if the probe is already at the destination, there is no direction to compute. Movement advances by one speed-scaled step along that direction, and destination checks should use a small numerical tolerance rather than exact floating-point equality.

Biosignature detection is probabilistic. A random detection score in the range `[0.0, 1.0)` is compared against the configured threshold; scores greater than or equal to the threshold count as detected. The higher-level detection action should tolerate arbitrary biosensor payloads without throwing.

Sensor integration should produce realistic bounded readings: temperature in `[-50, 50]`, humidity in `[0, 100]`, pressure in `[900, 1100]`, and a boolean biosensor value. Collected sensor data should use the keys `temperature`, `humidity`, `pressure`, and `biosensor`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate project-specific helper class required outside the UML-defined model.
```

# Operation Specifications
NuclearThermal::deactivate
Summary: Deactivates the nuclear-thermal propulsion unit.
Algorithm:
1. Set isActive = false.
Related Features: isActive
Related Classes: None
Input:
- None.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- None beyond object initialization.
Post-condition:
- isActive is false.

NuclearThermal::consumeFuel
Summary: Consumes fuel over time according to thrust, specific impulse, and gravity constant.
Algorithm:
1. If time <= 0 or drive is inactive, return current fuelMass.
2. Compute fuelConsumption = thrust * time / (specificImpulse * G), with G = 9.81.
3. Update fuelMass = max(0, fuelMass - fuelConsumption).
4. If fuelMass becomes 0, set isActive = false.
5. Return updated fuelMass.
Related Features: G, fuelMass, isActive, specificImpulse, thrust
Related Classes: None
Input:
- time: elapsed duration, expected non-negative.
Output:
- Updated fuel mass as a double, clamped to zero minimum.
Pre-condition:
- thrust, specificImpulse, fuelMass, and G are initialized and positive where required.
Post-condition:
- Fuel mass decreases according to the simplified model.
- Unit becomes inactive if fuel is exhausted.

NuclearThermal::status
Summary: Returns a status map describing the current nuclear-thermal propulsion state.
Algorithm:
1. Create a map.
2. Insert key is_active with current isActive.
3. Insert key thrust with current thrust.
4. Insert key fuel_mass with current fuelMass.
5. Insert key acceleration with calculateAcceleration() result.
6. Return the map.
Related Features: isActive, thrust, fuelMass, calculateAcceleration()
Related Classes: EMap
Input:
- None.
Output:
- An EMap containing:
  - is_active: boolean
  - thrust: double
  - fuel_mass: double
  - acceleration: double
Pre-condition:
- State fields are initialized.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.

NuclearThermal::simulate
Summary: Simulates nuclear-thermal propulsion over a time duration.
Algorithm:
1. If duration <= 0, do nothing.
2. Activate if fuelMass > 0.
3. Consume fuel for the given duration.
4. Deactivate if fuel is exhausted.
Related Features: G, fuelMass, isActive, specificImpulse, thrust, activate(), calculateAcceleration(), consumeFuel(), deactivate()
Related Classes: None
Input:
- duration: simulation duration in integer units, non-negative.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- Propulsion fields are initialized.
Post-condition:
- Propulsion state and fuel reflect the elapsed simulation interval.

NuclearThermal::calculateAcceleration
Summary: Computes propulsion acceleration from thrust and fuel mass while active.
Algorithm:
1. If isActive is false or fuelMass <= 0, return 0.
2. Otherwise return thrust / fuelMass.
Related Features: thrust, fuelMass, isActive
Related Classes: None
Input:
- Uses thrust, fuelMass, and isActive.
Output:
- A double acceleration value.
Pre-condition:
- thrust and fuelMass are finite.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.

NuclearThermal::activate
Summary: Activates the nuclear-thermal propulsion unit when fuel is available.
Algorithm:
1. Check fuelMass.
2. If fuelMass > 0, set isActive = true.
3. Otherwise set isActive = false.
Related Features: fuelMass, isActive
Related Classes: None
Input:
- Uses current fuelMass.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- fuelMass is initialized to a finite mass value.
Post-condition:
- isActive is true iff fuelMass is positive.

It covers communication reliability, laser and relay transmission, propulsion simulation, solar-sail thrust, autonomous probe navigation, biosignature detection, and sensor data collection. The common theme is lightweight simulation of spacecraft subsystems with deterministic return structures and simple physical models.

```text
## 2. Functional Requirement

Error correction uses a Hamming (7,4) style code. Encoding accepts exactly four binary characters and returns seven bits; invalid input should produce no encoded result. The four data bits occupy positions 1, 2, 4, and 6 of the encoded layout, while parity bits occupy positions 3, 5, and 7. Decoding accepts exactly seven received bits, corrects a single-bit error using the syndrome, and returns the corrected four data bits; invalid received data should produce no decoded result.

Laser communication models signal strength as power scaled by efficiency and linearly reduced by distance, using `power * efficiency / distance`. Transmission succeeds only when the resulting signal strength is above a small threshold of `0.01`.

Ion and nuclear-thermal propulsion share the same simplified operating model. A propulsion unit can activate only when fuel mass is positive. Acceleration is thrust divided by fuel mass while active and zero while inactive. Fuel consumption over time is based on thrust, specific impulse, and standard gravity `9.81`; if fuel would become negative, it should be clamped to zero and the unit should become inactive. Propulsion status maps should use the keys `is_active`, `thrust`, `fuel_mass`, and `acceleration`.

Solar-sail propulsion produces thrust only while deployed. The default solar constant is `1361`, and thrust is proportional to solar constant, sail area, and reflectivity, divided by the speed of light. A non-deployed sail has zero thrust. Solar-sail status maps should use the keys `is_deployed` and `thrust`.

Autonomous navigation works in two-dimensional space. Direction is the normalized vector from current position to destination; if the probe is already at the destination, there is no direction to compute. Movement advances by one speed-scaled step along that direction, and destination checks should use a small numerical tolerance rather than exact floating-point equality.

Biosignature detection is probabilistic. A random detection score in the range `[0.0, 1.0)` is compared against the configured threshold; scores greater than or equal to the threshold count as detected. The higher-level detection action should tolerate arbitrary biosensor payloads without throwing.

Sensor integration should produce realistic bounded readings: temperature in `[-50, 50]`, humidity in `[0, 100]`, pressure in `[900, 1100]`, and a boolean biosensor value. Collected sensor data should use the keys `temperature`, `humidity`, `pressure`, and `biosensor`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate project-specific helper class required outside the UML-defined model.
```

# Operation Specifications
NuclearThermal::deactivate
Summary: Deactivates the nuclear-thermal propulsion unit.
Algorithm:
1. Set isActive = false.
Related Features: isActive
Related Classes: None
Input:
- None.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- None beyond object initialization.
Post-condition:
- isActive is false.

NuclearThermal::consumeFuel
Summary: Consumes fuel over time according to thrust, specific impulse, and standard gravity.
Algorithm:
1. If time <= 0 or isActive is false, return current fuelMass.
2. Compute fuelConsumption = thrust * time / (specificImpulse * G), where G is the standard gravity constant 9.81.
3. Update fuelMass = max(0, fuelMass - fuelConsumption).
4. If fuelMass becomes 0, set isActive = false.
5. Return updated fuelMass.
Related Features: G, fuelMass, isActive, specificImpulse, thrust
Related Classes: None
Input:
- time: elapsed duration, a non-negative double.
Output:
- Updated fuel mass as a double, clamped to zero minimum.
Pre-condition:
- thrust, specificImpulse, fuelMass, and G are initialized and specificImpulse > 0.
Post-condition:
- Fuel mass decreases according to the simplified model.
- Unit becomes inactive if fuel is exhausted.

NuclearThermal::status
Summary: Returns a status map describing the current nuclear-thermal propulsion state.
Algorithm:
1. Create a map.
2. Insert key is_active with current isActive.
3. Insert key thrust with current thrust.
4. Insert key fuel_mass with current fuelMass.
5. Insert key acceleration with calculateAcceleration() result.
6. Return the map.
Related Features: isActive, thrust, fuelMass, calculateAcceleration()
Related Classes: EMap
Input:
- None.
Output:
- An EMap containing:
  - is_active: boolean
  - thrust: double
  - fuel_mass: double
  - acceleration: double
Pre-condition:
- State fields are initialized.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.

NuclearThermal::simulate
Summary: Simulates nuclear-thermal propulsion over a time duration.
Algorithm:
1. If duration <= 0, do nothing.
2. If fuelMass > 0, activate the unit; otherwise deactivate it.
3. If active, consume fuel for the given duration.
4. If fuel is exhausted, deactivate the unit.
Related Features: G, fuelMass, isActive, specificImpulse, thrust, activate(), deactivate(), consumeFuel()
Related Classes: None
Input:
- duration: simulation duration in integer units, non-negative.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- Propulsion fields are initialized.
Post-condition:
- Propulsion state and fuel reflect the elapsed simulation interval.

NuclearThermal::calculateAcceleration
Summary: Computes propulsion acceleration from thrust and fuel mass while active.
Algorithm:
1. If isActive is false or fuelMass <= 0, return 0.
2. Otherwise return thrust / fuelMass.
Related Features: thrust, fuelMass, isActive
Related Classes: None
Input:
- None.
Output:
- A double acceleration value.
Pre-condition:
- thrust and fuelMass are finite.
Post-condition:
- No state is modified.

NuclearThermal::activate
Summary: Activates the nuclear-thermal propulsion unit when fuel is available.
Algorithm:
1. Check fuelMass.
2. If fuelMass > 0, set isActive = true.
3. Otherwise set isActive = false.
Related Features: fuelMass, isActive
Related Classes: None
Input:
- None.
Output:
- No explicit return value.
Pre-condition:
- fuelMass is initialized to a finite mass value.
Post-condition:
- isActive is true iff fuelMass is positive.

SensorIntegration::collectData
Summary: Collects a bounded sensor snapshot containing temperature, humidity, pressure, and biosensor readings.
Algorithm:
1. Read temperature using readTemperature().
2. Read humidity using readHumidity().
3. Read pressure using readPressure().
4. Read biosensor using readBiosensor().

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_SpaceXplore-Core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system provides core algorithms for an interstellar exploration platform. It covers communication reliability, laser and relay transmission, propulsion simulation, solar-sail thrust, autonomous probe navigation, biosignature detection, and sensor data collection. The common theme is lightweight simulation of spacecraft subsystems with deterministic return structures and simple physical models.

## 2. Functional Requirement

Error correction uses a Hamming (7,4) style code. Encoding accepts exactly four binary characters and returns seven bits; invalid input should produce no encoded result. The four data bits occupy positions 1, 2, 4, and 6 of the encoded layout, while parity bits occupy positions 3, 5, and 7. Decoding accepts exactly seven received bits, corrects a single-bit error using the syndrome, and returns the corrected four data bits; invalid received data should produce no decoded result.

Laser communication models signal strength as power scaled by efficiency and linearly reduced by distance, using `power * efficiency / distance`. Transmission succeeds only when the resulting signal strength is above a small threshold of `0.01`.

Ion and nuclear-thermal propulsion share the same simplified operating model. A propulsion unit can activate only when fuel mass is positive. Acceleration is thrust divided by fuel mass while active and zero while inactive. Fuel consumption over time is based on thrust, specific impulse, and standard gravity `9.81`; if fuel would become negative, it should be clamped to zero and the unit should become inactive. Propulsion status maps should use the keys `is_active`, `thrust`, `fuel_mass`, and `acceleration`.

Solar-sail propulsion produces thrust only while deployed. The default solar constant is `1361`, and thrust is proportional to solar constant, sail area, and reflectivity, divided by the speed of light. A non-deployed sail has zero thrust. Solar-sail status maps should use the keys `is_deployed` and `thrust`.

Autonomous navigation works in two-dimensional space. Direction is the normalized vector from current position to destination; if the probe is already at the destination, there is no direction to compute. Movement advances by one speed-scaled step along that direction, and destination checks should use a small numerical tolerance rather than exact floating-point equality.

Biosignature detection is probabilistic. A random detection score in the range `[0.0, 1.0)` is compared against the configured threshold; scores greater than or equal to the threshold count as detected. The higher-level detection action should tolerate arbitrary biosensor payloads without throwing.

Sensor integration should produce realistic bounded readings: temperature in `[-50, 50]`, humidity in `[0, 100]`, pressure in `[900, 1100]`, and a boolean biosensor value. Collected sensor data should use the keys `temperature`, `humidity`, `pressure`, and `biosensor`.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate project-specific helper class required outside the UML-defined model.
```

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Activates the ion drive when sufficient fuel is available.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Check fuelMass.
   * 2. If fuelMass > 0, set isActive = true.
   * 3. Otherwise keep or set isActive = false.
   * Related Features: fuelMass, isActive
   * Related Classes: None
   * Input:
   * - Uses the current fuelMass field.
   * Output:
   * - No explicit return value.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - fuelMass is initialized to a finite mass value.
   * Post-condition:
   * - isActive is true if and only if fuelMass is positive.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void activate()
  {
    setIsActive(getFuelMass() > 0);
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Deactivates the ion drive.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Set isActive = false.
   * Related Features: isActive
   * Related Classes: None
   * Input:
   * - None.
   * Output:
   * - No explicit return value.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - None beyond object initialization.
   * Post-condition:
   * - isActive is false.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void deactivate()
  {
    setIsActive(false);
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Computes current acceleration from thrust and fuel mass while the drive is active.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If isActive is false or fuelMass <= 0, return 0.
   * 2. Otherwise compute acceleration = thrust / fuelMass.
   * Related Features: thrust, fuelMass, isActive
   * Related Classes: None
   * Input:
   * - Uses thrust, fuelMass, and isActive of the current object.
   * Output:
   * - A double acceleration value.
   * - Active state acceleration is thrust / fuelMass.
   * - Inactive state acceleration is 0.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - thrust and fuelMass must be finite numeric values.
   * Post-condition:
   * - No state is modified.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateAcceleration()
  {
    if (!isIsActive() || getFuelMass() <= 0)
    {
      return 0.0;
    }
    return getThrust() / getFuelMass();
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Reduces fuel according to thrust, specific impulse, standard gravity, and elapsed time, clamping fuel mass to zero and deactivating the drive when depleted.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If time <= 0, return current fuelMass.
   * 2. Compute fuelConsumption = thrust * time / (specificImpulse * G).
   * 3. Update fuelMass = max(0, fuelMass - fuelConsumption).
   * 4. If fuelMass becomes 0, set isActive = false.
   * 5. Return updated fuelMass.
   * Related Features: G, fuelMass, isActive, specificImpulse, thrust
   * Related Classes: None
   * Input:
   * - time: elapsed duration, expected to be non-negative.
   * Output:
   * - Updated fuel mass as a double.
   * - Never negative; clamped to zero.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - thrust, specificImpulse, fuelMass, and G must be initialized.
   * - specificImpulse and G must be positive.
   * Post-condition:
   * - fuelMass is decreased according to the simplified model.
   * - If fuel reaches zero, isActive becomes false.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double consumeFuel(final double time)
  {
    if (time <= 0)
    {
      return getFuelMass();
    }
    double fuelConsumption = getThrust() * time / (getSpecificImpulse() * getG());
    double updatedFuelMass = Math.max(0.0, getFuelMass() - fuelConsumption);
    setFuelMass(updatedFuelMass);
    if (updatedFuelMass == 0.0)
    {
      setIsActive(false);
    }
    return updatedFuelMass;
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Returns a status map describing the current ion drive state.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. Create a map.
   * 2. Insert key is_active with current isActive.
   * 3. Insert key thrust with current thrust.
   * 4. Insert key fuel_mass with current fuelMass.
   * 5. Insert key acceleration with calculateAcceleration() result.
   * 6. Return the map.
   * Related Features: isActive, thrust, fuelMass, calculateAcceleration()
   * Related Classes: EMap
   * Input:
   * - None.
   * Output:
   * - An EMap containing:
   *   - is_active: boolean
   *   - thrust: double
   *   - fuel_mass: double
   *   - acceleration: double
   * Pre-condition:
   * - The object fields are initialized.
   * Post-condition:
   * - No state is modified.
   * - Returned map uses the required keys.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public Map<String, Object> status()
  {
    Map<String, Object> status = new HashMap<String, Object>();
    status.put("is_active", Boolean.valueOf(isIsActive()));
    status.put("thrust", Double.valueOf(getThrust()));
    status.put("fuel_mass", Double.valueOf(getFuelMass()));
    status.put("acceleration", Double.valueOf(calculateAcceleration()));
    return status;
    
  }

  /**
   * <!-- begin-user-doc -->
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * Summary: Simulates ion drive behavior for the given duration using activation, acceleration, and fuel consumption rules.
   * Algorithm:
   * 1. If duration <= 0, do nothing.
   * 2. If fuelMass > 0, set isActive = true; otherwise set isActive = false.
   * 3. If isActive is true, update fuelMass by calling consumeFuel(duration).
   * 4. If fuelMass becomes 0 after consumption, deactivate the drive.
   * 5. Acceleration is determined by calculateAcceleration() when needed by the simulation logic.
   * Related Features: G, fuelMass, isActive, specificImpulse, thrust, activate(), calculateAcceleration(), consumeFuel(), deactivate()
   * Related Classes: None
   * Input:
   * - duration: elapsed simulation time in integer units, expected to be non-negative.
   * Output:
   * - No explicit return value.
   * Pre-condition:
   * - The propulsion object must have initialized thrust, specificImpulse, fuelMass, and G.
   * Post-condition:
   * - Fuel and active state may change according to the simplified propulsion model.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public void simulate(final int duration)
  {
    if (duration <= 0)
    {
      return;
    }
    if (getFuelMass() > 0)
    {
      activate();
    }
    else
    {
      deactivate();
    }
    if (isIsActive())
    {
      consumeFuel(duration);
      if (getFuelMass() == 0.0)
      {
        deactivate();
      }
    }
    
  }

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.

  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.

  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
